React limits the number of  \r\n  nested updates to prevent infinite loops.                                                                                                                                      \r\n      at setRef (composeRefs.tsx:11:5)                                                                                                                                           \r\n                                                                                               \r\n      ...                                                                                                                                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  The setRef function in @radix-ui/react-compose-refs recursively triggers itself, causing React to hit the maximum update depth limit.                                          \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  In React 19, ref callback behavior changed - ref callbacks can now return cleanup functions, and React may call ref callbacks differently than in React 18. When setRef calls  \r\n  ref(value) at line 11, it triggers a re-render which invokes setRef again via the composed ref callback at line 22, creating an infinite loop.    \r\n\r\n\r\n### Expected behavior\r\n\r\n  Composed refs should work without causing infinite update loops in React 19.                                                                                                    \r\n\r\n### Reproducible example\r\n\r\nI have platejs plugins with radix popovers and radix tooltips on them. If I write fast inside plate, the page breaks. \r\nI\'m on a Macbook M1 Pro with 32 GB. Possible approaches:                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  1. Memoize the composed ref callback to prevent unnecessary re-invocations                                                                                                     \r\n  2. Add a guard to prevent recursive setRef calls                                                                                                                               \r\n  3. Handle the cleanup function return value from React 19 ref callbacks       \r\n\r\n### Additional context\r\nRight now, it works fine with react 18 (even on next.js 16+) and we had to downgrade.\r\n\r\nI also saw this reddit post https://www.reddit.com/r/react/comments/1nnvwsp/react_19_causes_maximum_update_depth_exceeded/ where a user raised a similar case\r\n\r\nReact 19 introduced changes to ref callback behavior, including support for cleanup functions returned from ref callbacks. This may be causing the ref to be re-invoked on each render cycle. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'The upstream fix (PR #3899) is as follows:\n1. Inside `slot.tsx`: replace the `composeRefs` import with `useComposedRefs`, and move `slottableElementRef`/`composedRef` to before the error-return branch, using `useComposedRefs` (memoized via useCallback) so that the composed ref callback stays stable across renders.\n2. Add regression test file `slot-ref-stability.test.tsx`.\n3. Add changeset.\n\nRoot cause: Slot was creating a new `composeRefs(...)` callback on every render. In React 19, when a new ref callback is attached on every render, React calls the old ref with null (or the cleanup) and then calls the new ref — for components whose refs trigger state updates (like Slate/Plate editors that setState in response to ref changes), this can cause an infinite loop → "Maximum update depth exceeded." Making the composed ref stable (memoized) prevents the re-invocation loop.\n\nWait, but `useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` — `slottableElementRef` is the child\'s own ref, and it may be a new function on every render (e.g., if the child passes a ref callback that gets recreated on every render). `useComposedRefs` uses `useCallback(composeRefs(...refs), refs)` — the dependency array `refs` is `[forwardedRef, slottableElementRef]`... `useCallback(fn, deps)` with deps = the `refs` array — hmm, the actual upstream implementation is:\n\n```ts\nfunction useComposedRefs<T>(...refs: PossibleRef<T>[]): React.RefCallback<T> {\n  // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps\n  return React.useCallback(composeRefs(...refs), refs);\n}\n```\n\nThe deps array is `refs` itself (the array of ref values). React\'s `useCallback(fn, deps)` compares deps with a shallow equality check: if `forwardedRef` is a stable object (a ref object) and `slottableElementRef` is stable, the composed ref stays stable. If the child\'s ref callback is recreated every render, the composed ref will be recreated too — but that\'s unavoidable, and the bug is specifically about Slot itself creating a new callback even when all inputs are stable.\n\nLet me read the full raw diff from artifact://20 to confirm the exact placement of the fix (the hook call must be unconditional — before the early `return children;` branch — to comply with the rules of hooks!). This is an important detail: in the current code, `slottableElementRef`/`composedRefs` are computed after the early-return branch (`if (!slottableElement) { ... return children; }`). The fix moves the `useComposedRefs` call before the early return to keep the hook order stable.
